# MJExplorer Regression Suite Redesign — Coverage Plan

> **Status:** ✅ APPLIED to the working tree (not yet committed). 307 new `.T*.json` tests generated
> (T232–T538), 156 redundant tests deleted (104 cut + 52 merged), `.regression-suite.json` rebuilt to
> 380 members, and 4 temporary sub-suites pruned of deleted-test references. All 380 test files + 10
> suite files validate as JSON with zero dangling `@lookup` references.
> Generated by a 45-agent discovery workflow that read the actual component code of every MJExplorer application.
>
> **To review:** `git status metadata/` — 307 untracked (new), 156 deleted, 5 modified (suites).
> **To ship:** `npx mj sync push --dir=metadata` (validates + assigns IDs/checksums), then commit.
> **To revert everything:** `git checkout -- metadata/ && git clean -f metadata/tests/regression/`.
>
> The suite is renumbered **T001–T380** (contiguous, no gaps): the 73 kept survivors are T001–T073
> (in original order), and the 307 new tests below are **T074–T380** — provisional `N00k` maps to
> `T0{73+k}` (N001 → T074, … N307 → T380).

## TL;DR

The current 229-test Computer-Use suite over-indexes on **data-existence** ("open entity X, verify rows appear") — repeating the *same* generic-grid/form/query code path once per entity, which adds data coverage but **zero code coverage**. This plan replaces that with a suite of **functionally-distinct** tests, each exercising a different feature/behavior and written to be **data-agnostic** (pass on empty-states, assert behavior not row counts).

| | Count |
|---|---|
| Existing tests audited | 229 |
| → **Kept** (genuinely distinct) | **73** |
| → Merged into a survivor | 52 |
| → **Cut** (per-entity / data-existence dupes) | **104** |
| **New distinct tests designed** | **307** |
| **Grand total (planned suite)** | **380** |

The suite goes from **229 tests / 8 apps** to **380 tests / 28 apps** — while *removing* 156 redundant tests. Net new coverage lands almost entirely on the ~20 applications that had **zero** tests before (Chat, Knowledge Hub, Predictive Studio, Testing, Credentials, Actions, Component Studio, Version History, Scheduling, Routines, Bulk Operations, Permissions, File Browser, MCP, Realtime, Archiving) plus deep feature coverage of the entity-viewer grid, forms, and admin subsystems.

---

## The problem, restated

MJExplorer's Data Explorer grid is **one generic, metadata-driven component**. Browsing `Events` vs `Courses` vs `Organizations` runs identical TypeScript — only the metadata and the rows differ. The same is true of the generic entity **form**, the **query runner**, the **related-records** grid, and **search/filter/sort**. So the ~100 "per-entity" tests in the old suite collapse to a handful of representatives; the rest were testing *whether the seed database has data*, not *whether the feature works*.

## The method

1. **Audit** — one agent classified all 229 existing tests into keep / merge / cut against a strict "functionality-not-data" rubric, and produced the list of features already covered (the dedup baseline).
2. **Discover** — 30 agents (one per app / cross-cutting area) read the real component & template code, enumerated genuinely-distinct features, and drafted one data-agnostic test per feature — each grounded in a specific source file to prevent inventing features.
3. **Critique** — 8 agents ran a cross-lane dedup + data-existence gate and rewrote weak goals/criteria.
4. **Write** — assembled into this reviewable document.

Every new test spec below carries: **Goal** (the computer-use task), **Pass criteria** (what the LLM judge checks), **Distinct** (the unique code path it hits), and **Grounding** (the source file proving the feature exists).

## Consolidation plan for the existing 229

### Cut — 104 redundant per-entity / data-existence duplicates

| # | Tests | Why (shared code path) |
|---|---|---|
| 31 | T26–T34, T69, T71, T73, T75, T79, T85, T89, T151–T165 | Per-entity **"browse grid X"** — identical generic entity grid. Keep T04 (populated) + T200 (system/wide) as representatives. |
| 23 | T63–T65, T70, T72, T74, T76, T78, T80–T82, T84, T86–T88, T181–T188 | Per-entity **related-records / drilldown** — same related-grid-on-form component. Keep T62 (section render) + T214 (FK nav). |
| 13 | T07, T48–T52, T77, T83, T166–T170 | Per-entity **view-form** — identical CodeGen generic form render. Keep T05. |
| 7 | T10, T58, T59, T60, T201, T203, T209 | Plain **edit+save** dupes — same edit/Save path. Keep T206 (round-trip) + T202/T207/T208 (distinct field editors). |
| 7 | T93–T99 | Per-query **"run query X"** — same query-runner. Keep T11 + T100 (params) + T102 (result link). |
| 5 | T06, T36, T37, T38, T172 | Per-entity **grid quick-search** — same search box. Keep T35. |
| 5 | T40, T41, T171, T173, T174 | Per-entity **grid filter** — same filter path. Keep T39. |
| 5 | T43, T44, T175, T176, T177 | Per-entity **single-column sort** — same sort code. Keep T42. |
| 5 | T09, T54, T55, T56, T57 | Per-entity **create-record** — same create+Save form. Keep T53. |
| 2 | T179, T180 | Open-entity-via-sidebar-search — already covered by T04/T47. |
| 1 | T178 | Pagination dupe — same paging as T45. |

### Merge — 26 groups (52 tests folded into a surviving representative)

### Keep — 73 genuinely-distinct survivors

Login/shell (T01, T03, T08, T23, T24), generic-grid representatives (T04, T200), record open/form (T05, T92), grid features (T35, T39, T42, T45, T46, T47, T220–T224, T227), CRUD & fields (T53, T66, T67, T202, T204–T208), record features (T210–T214, T230), queries (T11, T100, T102), dashboards/home (T103, T105, T110), AI (T111–T114, T116, T118, T119, T218, T219, T231), Admin (T123–T126, T128–T130, T132, T192–T194), and app-nav representatives (T133, T139, T143, T144, T147, T198, T17). # New Distinct Tests (307)

*Each spec is a proposed Computer-Use test. Numbered N001–N307 (provisional). These map 1:1 to `.T*.json` files to be generated on approval.*


## Data Explorer & Records

### data-explorer-grid

- **[N001] Grid View-Type Switch to Cards** `(P2, grid)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and go to Data Explorer, then its Data section. Open the 'Products' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type 'Products', click the exact match). Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. Once the records grid renders, find the view-type switcher dropdown in the view toolbar at the top of the records area (a button showing the current type like 'Grid' with a chevron, next to the saved-view selector). Open it and choose 'Cards'. Success is the records re-rendering as a grid of cards instead of a table — you do NOT need to interact with the cards.
  - **Pass criteria:** The view-type switcher dropdown opens and lists more than one view type, including a 'Cards' option; After selecting Cards, the records re-render as cards (a card grid) rather than the tabular AG-Grid, OR a valid empty-state is shown for that view; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the cards-view-renderer plug-in code path, which is entirely separate from the default AG-Grid table renderer; switching renderers is not touched by any existing grid test.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/entity-viewer/src/lib/view-type-switcher/view-type-switcher.component.html (view-type-dropdown, SelectViewType) + view-types/renderers/cards-view-renderer.component.ts

- **[N002] Saved View Selector Browse and Select** `(P2, grid)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and go to Data Explorer > Data. Open the 'Products' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type 'Products', click the exact match). Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. In the view toolbar at the top-left of the records area, click the saved-view selector button (a layer-group icon labeled with the current view name or '(Default)') to open the Views panel. Verify the panel opens listing at least a '(Default)' option (plus any 'My Views' / 'Shared With Me' sections). Click the '(Default)' option (or any listed view). Success is the panel opening and the chosen option applying (a checkmark on it and the grid reloading).
  - **Pass criteria:** Clicking the saved-view selector opens a Views panel showing at least the '(Default)' entry; Selecting a view applies it — a check indicator appears on the chosen view and the grid reloads (or a valid empty-state renders); No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the saved-view (mj-view-selector) read/select lifecycle over the User Views entity — distinct from saved QUERIES (already covered) and from all AG-Grid interactions.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/entity-viewer/src/lib/view-selector/view-selector.component.html (vs-panel, vs-card, SelectDefault/SelectView)

- **[N003] Create New Saved View Round-Trip** `(P2, grid)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and go to Data Explorer > Data. Open the 'Products' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type 'Products', click the exact match). Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. Open the saved-view selector (layer-group icon) and click 'New View' in the panel footer (this opens a quick-save / configure dialog). Enter a unique view name such as 'CU Grid Test 4821' and confirm creation (Create View / Save). Success is the new view being created and becoming the active view — its name shows on the selector button and/or appears under 'My Views'. You do NOT need to configure columns or filters; naming and saving is enough.
  - **Pass criteria:** A create/quick-save dialog appears with an editable view-name field after clicking 'New View'; After saving, the newly named view becomes the selected view (name shown on selector button) or appears in the 'My Views' list; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the view CREATE + server-persist path (quick-save dialog / config-panel Settings tab -> OnSaveAsNew), a write round-trip distinct from read-only view selection and from record CRUD.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/entity-viewer/src/lib/view-selector/view-selector.component.html (OnQuickSave 'New View') + view-workspace/quick-save-dialog + view-config-panel/view-config-panel.component.html (Settings tab, ViewName, OnSaveAsNew)

- **[N004] Configure View Smart Filter Mode** `(P2, grid)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and go to Data Explorer > Data. Open the 'Products' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type 'Products', click the exact match). Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. Open Configure View (the sliders / sliders-h icon in the view toolbar near the saved-view selector). Click the 'Filters' tab. It offers a filter-mode selector with two choices: 'Smart Filter' (natural language) and 'Traditional Filter'. Choose 'Smart Filter'. Success is the smart-filter UI rendering: a natural-language description textarea plus example chips (e.g. 'Last 30 days', 'Active only'). Seeing the smart-filter input render is success — you do NOT need to type a prompt or run it.
  - **Pass criteria:** The Configure View panel opens and the Filters tab shows a Smart vs Traditional filter-mode selector; Selecting 'Smart Filter' reveals a natural-language textarea and example prompt chips; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Smart Filter (AI/natural-language) is a separate filter branch from the Traditional field/operator/value builder that the existing advanced-filter test covers.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/entity-viewer/src/lib/view-config-panel/view-config-panel.component.html (Filters tab, FilterMode 'smart', SmartFilterPrompt textarea + example-chip buttons)

- **[N005] Grid Recycle Bin Restore Panel** `(P2, grid)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and go to Data Explorer > Data. Open the 'Products' entity (or 'Events') via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type the name, click the exact match). Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. In the grid toolbar (top-right area), look for the Recycle Bin chip (a trash-can-arrow-up icon). If present, click it. Success is the Recycle Bin slide-in panel opening and showing either a list of deleted records each with a 'Restore' button, or a valid 'Recycle Bin is empty' state. Seeing the panel render is success — you do NOT need to actually restore anything. If the chip is not visible (no deleted records or no Delete permission for this entity), that is an acceptable outcome.
  - **Pass criteria:** The Recycle Bin chip opens a slide-in panel titled 'Recycle Bin', OR the chip is legitimately absent (no deletions / no permission); When the panel opens it shows deleted-record entries with Restore buttons OR a valid empty-state ('Recycle Bin is empty'); No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the soft-delete / recycle-bin restore path driven by Record Changes snapshots — a code path unrelated to grid rendering, filtering, or standard CRUD.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/entity-viewer/src/lib/entity-data-grid/entity-data-grid.component.html (mj-recycle-bin-chip) + recycle-bin/recycle-bin.component.html (Restore button, mj-restore-preview-panel, empty state)

- **[N006] Configure View Column Format Editor** `(P3, grid)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and go to Data Explorer > Data. Open the 'Products' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type 'Products', click the exact match). Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. Open Configure View (sliders icon in the view toolbar) and stay on the 'Columns' tab. Each visible column row has a paintbrush 'Format column' button — click it for any column. Success is the column format-editor sub-panel opening with a 'Format Type' dropdown (Text / Number / Currency / Date / Boolean / etc.), an alignment toggle, header/cell style buttons, and a live Preview. Seeing the format editor render is success — you do NOT need to save formatting.
  - **Pass criteria:** Clicking a column's paintbrush button opens the format-editor sub-panel; The editor shows a Format Type selector plus alignment/style controls and a Preview section; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Per-column formatting (type/decimals/currency/date-format/alignment/colors + live preview) is a distinct rich sub-feature; existing column-management coverage only exercises show/hide/reorder.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/entity-viewer/src/lib/view-config-panel/view-config-panel.component.html (Columns tab OpenFormatEditor -> format-editor: Format Type select, alignment-toggle, style-buttons, preview-table)

- **[N007] Configure View Add Aggregate Dialog** `(P3, grid)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and go to Data Explorer > Data. Open the 'Products' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type 'Products', click the exact match). Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. Open Configure View (sliders icon) and click the 'Aggregates' tab. Click the 'Add Aggregate' button. Success is the aggregate setup dialog opening, letting you choose an aggregate function (sum / average / count / etc.) and a field, plus a display type (card vs column footer). Seeing the setup dialog render is success — you do NOT need to save the aggregate.
  - **Pass criteria:** The Aggregates tab renders with an 'Add Aggregate' button; Clicking it opens an aggregate setup dialog offering a function/field choice and a display-type option; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises aggregate CREATION via the setup dialog (function + field + display type), distinct from the existing test that only verifies an aggregate summary row renders.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/entity-viewer/src/lib/view-config-panel/view-config-panel.component.html (Aggregates tab OpenAddAggregateDialog -> mj-aggregate-setup-dialog) + aggregate-setup-dialog/aggregate-setup-dialog.component.ts

- **[N008] Grid View-Type Switch to Timeline** `(P3, grid)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and go to Data Explorer > Data. Open the 'Events' entity (or 'Products') via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type the name, click the exact match). Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. Open the view-type switcher dropdown in the view toolbar. If 'Timeline' is offered, select it and confirm the records re-render along a chronological timeline axis. (Timeline only appears for entities that expose a date field; if it is not offered, selecting another type such as 'Cards' and seeing the view re-render is an acceptable outcome.)
  - **Pass criteria:** The view-type switcher lists multiple types; if 'Timeline' is offered, selecting it renders a chronological/timeline layout; If Timeline is not available, an alternate type (e.g. Cards) is selectable and the view re-renders, OR a valid empty-state shows; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** The timeline-view-renderer plots records on a date axis — a distinct renderer code path from both the AG-Grid table and the cards renderer, gated by the entity having a date field.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/entity-viewer/src/lib/view-type-switcher/view-type-switcher.component.html + view-types/renderers/timeline-view-renderer.component.ts + view-types/descriptors/timeline-view-type.ts (IsAvailableFor date field)

- **[N009] Open Saved View In New Tab** `(P3, grid)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and go to Data Explorer > Data. Open the 'Products' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type 'Products', click the exact match). Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. Open the saved-view selector (layer-group icon). On a listed view card (or the '(Default)' entry once selected) use the 'Open in Tab' action (an external-link / up-right-from-square icon on the view card, or the icon button next to the selector). Success is the view opening as its own workspace tab/resource. Seeing the view open as a distinct tab is success.
  - **Pass criteria:** An 'Open in Tab' control (external-link icon) is available for a view in the selector; Activating it opens the view as its own tab/resource showing the entity's records (or a valid empty-state); No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the view-as-resource-tab navigation path (OpenViewInTabRequested), distinct from opening a record tab and from selecting a view in place.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/entity-viewer/src/lib/view-selector/view-selector.component.html (OnOpenViewInTab / OnOpenInTab) + view-workspace/view-workspace.component.html (OpenViewInTabRequested)

- **[N010] Grid Row Multi-Selection State** `(P3, grid)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and go to Data Explorer > Data. Open the 'Products' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type 'Products', click the exact match). Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. In the records grid, select one or more rows using their row checkboxes (use the header select-all checkbox to select multiple if rows are present). Success is the grid reflecting the selection — a selection count appears (e.g. '(1 selected)' / '(2 selected)') and/or selection-dependent toolbar actions (such as Add to List / Delete) become enabled. If the grid is empty, a valid empty-state is acceptable. You do NOT need to run any of those actions.
  - **Pass criteria:** Selecting one or more rows via the checkboxes updates the grid selection state — a selection count and/or highlighted rows appear (or a valid empty-state renders if there are no rows); At least one selection-dependent toolbar control becomes enabled once a row is selected, OR the selection count is clearly displayed; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Isolates the grid row-selection model (selection count + selection-gated toolbar enablement); existing bulk tests jump straight into the Add-to-List dialog rather than verifying the selection mechanism itself.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/entity-viewer/src/lib/entity-data-grid/entity-data-grid.component.html (agRowSelection, onAgSelectionChanged, selection-count span, HasSelection/HasMultipleSelection-gated buttons)

- **[N011] Reset Modified View To Default** `(P3, grid)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and go to Data Explorer > Data. Open the 'Products' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type 'Products', click the exact match). Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. Make an in-grid modification that marks the view as changed — for example click a column header to change the sort, or type into the 'Filter records...' box. Notice the saved-view selector shows a 'modified' indicator (a dot next to the view name). Open the saved-view selector panel and click 'Reset to Default'. Success is the view reverting to its default state and the modified indicator clearing.
  - **Pass criteria:** After a sort/filter change the saved-view selector shows a modified indicator (unsaved-changes dot); Clicking 'Reset to Default' reverts the grid to the default view and the modified indicator clears (grid reloads or shows a valid empty-state); No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the view revert/reset-to-default path (discarding unsaved view modifications), distinct from selecting or creating a view.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/entity-viewer/src/lib/view-selector/view-selector.component.html (vs-modified-dot, OnResetToDefault 'Reset to Default') + view-workspace RevertRequested

### data-explorer-forms

- **[N012] Entity Form FK Field Typeahead Search and Select** `(P1, form)`
  - **Goal:** Open the 'Event Registrations' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box (type 'Event Registrations', click the exact match), click any row, then click 'Open Full Record'. Click the Edit (pencil) button to enter edit mode. Find a foreign-key field (e.g. 'Member' or 'Event') — it renders as a text box with a magnifying-glass icon. Click it and type a few characters; a dropdown of matching related records should appear. The success condition is that the FK autocomplete dropdown renders search results (a results grid/list) OR shows a clear 'No matches' state as you type — you do NOT need to actually save. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away.
  - **Pass criteria:** In edit mode, a foreign-key field renders as a searchable text input with a magnifying-glass icon (not a plain dropdown); Typing into the FK field triggers a dropdown that shows matching related-record suggestions OR a clear 'No matches' empty state; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the mj-fk-search typeahead autocomplete in EDIT mode (async related-record search + suggestion dropdown) — a completely different code path from read-mode FK link navigation (T214) and FK display-name rendering (T92), and from the static <select> dropdown editor (T207).
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/base-forms/src/lib/field/form-field.component.html (@if HasRelatedEntity → div.mj-fk-search input with OnFKInput/OnFKFocus, ShowFKDropdown, FKSuggestions grid); form-field.component.ts OnFKInput/FKSuggestions

- **[N013] Entity Form FK Search Inline Create-New Footer** `(P3, form)`
  - **Goal:** Open the 'Event Registrations' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box, open a record's full form, and click Edit to enter edit mode. Click a foreign-key field (e.g. 'Member') and type a value unlikely to match anything (e.g. 'ZZQX Nonexistent'). At the bottom of the FK search dropdown a sticky 'Create new...' footer button (with a plus icon), prefilled with the typed text, should appear. Success is seeing that 'Create new' footer render (you do NOT click it), OR — if the current user lacks create permission on the related entity — the footer being deliberately absent while the dropdown still shows a no-match state. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away.
  - **Pass criteria:** Typing a non-matching value in a foreign-key field renders the FK search dropdown; A 'Create new...' footer button with a plus icon appears at the bottom of the dropdown, prefilled with the typed text (OR is deliberately absent when the user lacks create permission on the related entity); No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Targets the CanCreateFK create-new footer inside the FK search dropdown — a distinct permission-gated affordance separate from the search itself and from the New-Record button on the grid.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/base-forms/src/lib/field/form-field.component.html lines ~284-289 (@if CanCreateFK → button.mj-fk-create-footer OnFKCreateNew, FKCreateLabel)

- **[N014] Entity Form FK Search Field-Scope and Column Picker** `(P3, form)`
  - **Goal:** Open the 'Event Registrations' entity via the LEFT sidebar 'Search entities...' box, open a record's full form, and click Edit. Click a foreign-key field to focus it — a small scope 'pill' (magnifying-glass + field name + chevron) may appear next to the input when the related entity has more than one searchable field. Click that pill to open a menu listing which field is being searched and column show/hide (eye) toggles. Success is seeing EITHER the scope/column picker menu render when the pill is clicked, OR (if the related entity has only one searchable field) the FK results dropdown with sortable column headers. You do NOT need to change or save anything. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away.

Success is that expanding a server card reveals its expanded tools section; if no servers are configured, a 'No MCP servers configured' empty state is an acceptable outcome. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away.
  - **Pass criteria:** The Servers tab shows server cards, OR a valid 'No MCP servers configured' empty state; Clicking a server card header expands it to show detail fields (Transport, Auth, Connections, Tools) and an 'Available Tools' section (when at least one server exists); The expand arrow/state visibly toggles when the card is expanded and collapsed; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages

If the queue is empty, confirming the "All caught up!" empty state renders correctly is also success. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away.

## Admin & Governance

### admin-identity

- **[N103] API Key Creation Wizard** `(P1, admin)`
  - **Goal:** Assume you are already logged in at http://localhost:4200. Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo (NOT the Ctrl+K search box) and switch to the Admin application, then open 'Identity & Access' and select the 'API Keys' sub-section from the left navigation. Click the 'Generate New Key' button (top-right, or the 'Generate Key' quick action). A slide-out wizard titled 'Generate New API Key' should open on step 1 (Configure). Type a label such as 'Regression Test Key' in the Key Label field, then click 'Next: Permissions' to advance to step 2 where permission scope categories are listed. Seeing the wizard advance to the Permissions step is success — you do NOT need to actually generate the key. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away.
  - **Pass criteria:** The 'Generate New API Key' slide-out opens showing step 1 (Configure) with a Key Label field and a step indicator (Configure / Permissions); After typing a label and clicking 'Next: Permissions', the wizard advances to the scope-selection step showing permission scope categories (or a loading/empty scopes state); The step indicator reflects the move from step 1 to step 2; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the bespoke two-step slide-out wizard (Step 'configure' -> 'scopes' via goToScopes()) with a step indicator and scope-category picker — a custom flow entirely separate from the generic entity create form.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Explorer/dashboards/src/APIKeys/api-key-create-dialog.component.html (Step==='configure'/'scopes', goToScopes(), step-indicator); api-keys-resource.component.html openCreateDialog()

  - **Pass criteria:** The App State inspector shows a section tab-nav and a read-only JSON snapshot in a code editor; Selecting a different section tab updates the section title and swaps the JSON content shown, confirming section navigation works; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the tab-nav-driven read-only snapshot switching (onTabChange + StateJson) - the runtime-state snapshot inspector, a distinct data source and interaction not covered by any other DevTools test.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Explorer/dashboards/src/DevTools/app-state-inspector.component.html (mj-tab-nav [Tabs]=tabsConfig (TabChange)=onTabChange, SectionLabel, mj-code-editor [readonly]=true StateJson)

  - **Goal:** You are logged in at http://localhost:4200. Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and choose the 'Permissions' application. It opens on the 'User Access' tab in the left nav. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away. Success is seeing the 'User Access Report' surface render: a 'User:' picker dropdown at top (pre-selected to a user, since the current user is auto-selected on load) and either a summary line reading 'N permissions across M domains' with collapsible domain accordion panels below, OR a clear empty-state card ('No permissions' / 'No user selected'). You do NOT need to interact further — a rendered report or a valid empty-state is success.
  - **Pass criteria:** The Permissions app opens and the User Access Report renders with a 'User:' selector dropdown at the top; A user appears pre-selected and the page shows either a '...permissions across ...domains' summary with domain accordion panels OR a clearly labeled empty-state card; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the custom PermissionsUserAccessResource component's ngOnInit path — auto-selecting the current user and calling PermissionEngine.GetAllUserPermissions to aggregate effective permissions across all domains, then grouping them into accordion panels. This is a bespoke read-only aggregation surface, not the generic entity grid.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Explorer/dashboards/src/Permissions/user-access-resource.component.ts (loadUsers() auto-selects md.CurrentUser, loadPermissionsForSelectedUser() calls PermissionEngine.Instance.GetAllUserPermissions) + user-access-resource.component.html (selector-row #user-select, domain-groups mj-accordion-panel, total-summary)

Pages can take 10-30s to load — wait, don't navigate away. Open the 'User:' dropdown at the top and select a DIFFERENT user than the one currently selected. Success is that the report RESPONDS to the change: a brief 'Loading permissions...' state may appear, then the domain accordion groups / summary count update to reflect the newly selected user (or a valid 'No permissions' empty-state appears for that user). If the user has assigned roles, a roles badge (user-tag icon) shows their role names next to the picker. Seeing the report re-query and re-render for the new user is success.

Different code branch from the initial current-user load (resolvePermissionsUser role-fetch path) and distinct from any generic grid interaction.

Open the Permissions app via the application switcher next to the logo and stay on the 'User Access' tab; if no domains show for the pre-selected user, pick a user in the 'User:' dropdown who has permissions (an admin user is a good bet). Pages can take 10-30s to load — wait, don't navigate away. Find a collapsible domain accordion panel (each shows a domain name with an icon and a numeric count badge on the right) and click its header to expand it. - **[N149] Resource Access Lookup Requires Type And ID** `(P2, permissions)`
  - **Goal:** You are logged in at http://localhost:4200. Open the Permissions app via the application switcher next to the logo, then click 'Resource Access' in the left nav. Pages can take 10-30s to load — wait, don't navigate away. Without filling in the 'Resource Type:' combobox and 'Resource ID:' field (leave at least one blank), click the 'Lookup' button. Success is that the lookup is BLOCKED with a validation error message stating that resource type and resource ID are both required (an error alert appears at the top of the page) — the grantee results table does NOT appear. Seeing the guard prevent the empty lookup with a clear message is success.
  - **Pass criteria:** Clicking 'Lookup' with a blank Resource Type and/or Resource ID surfaces a validation error message indicating both fields are required; No grantee results table is shown for the empty/invalid lookup; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages (the validation notice is expected and is not a crash)
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the client-side required-field guard in OnLookupResource ('Resource type and resource ID are both required.') before any engine call. This is a distinct validation-block behavior, data-agnostic, and separate from the successful-lookup path.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Explorer/dashboards/src/Permissions/resource-access-resource.component.ts (OnLookupResource guard: if (!this.ResourceTypeInput.trim() || !this.ResourceIdInput.trim()) { this.ErrorMessage = 'Resource type and resource ID are both required.' }) + resource-access-resource.component.html (mj-alert Variant="error", Lookup button)

- **[N150] Resource Access Lookup Shows Grantees Or No Results** `(P2, permissions)`
  - **Goal:** You are logged in at http://localhost:4200. Open the Permissions app via the application switcher next to the logo, then click 'Resource Access' in the left nav. Pages can take 10-30s to load — wait, don't navigate away. Pick a Domain, choose or type a value in the 'Resource Type:' combobox, and enter any plausible identifier in the 'Resource ID:' field (a UUID-shaped string is fine even if it may not match a real record). Click 'Lookup'. Success is that the query RUNS and the surface shows one of two valid outcomes: a grantee table with columns Grantee, Type, Actions, Effect, and Expires plus a summary count, OR a 'No grantees found' no-results empty-state that echoes your query. Either a populated result table or the no-match state proves the lookup feature works.
  - **Pass criteria:** After entering a domain, resource type, and resource ID and clicking Lookup, the surface transitions out of the initial 'No lookup yet' state; The result is either a grantee table (Grantee / Type / Actions / Effect / Expires columns) with a count, OR a clearly labeled 'No grantees found' empty-state referencing the query; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises OnLookupResource -> PermissionEngine.GetResourcePermissions, which resolves WHO can access a specific (domain, resource type, resource ID) triple and renders the grantee table. This grantee-resolution path is distinct from User Access's per-user aggregation and from Audit Log's timeline.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Explorer/dashboards/src/Permissions/resource-access-resource.component.ts (OnLookupResource, GetResourcePermissions, NoGranteesMessage) + resource-access-resource.component.html (perms-table Grantee/Type/Actions/Effect/Expires, empty-state Variant="no-results")

- **[N151] Audit Log Timeline Query Renders Entries** `(P1, permissions)`
  - **Goal:** You are logged in at http://localhost:4200. Open the Permissions app via the application switcher next to the logo, then click 'Audit Log' in the left nav. Pages can take 10-30s to load — wait, don't navigate away. Initially you should see a 'No query yet' empty-state. Optionally set a Domain filter or leave all filters (Domain, Changed by, From, To) at their defaults, then click the 'Query' button. Success is that the timeline query RUNS and shows one of two valid outcomes: an entries table with columns When, Who, Change, Domain, Entity, Record, and Summary (each Change cell shows a change-type chip like Create/Update/Delete with an icon) plus an entry count, OR a 'No audit entries found' no-results empty-state. Either outcome proves the query feature works.
  - **Pass criteria:** The Audit Log surface shows a 'No query yet' state before querying, and clicking 'Query' transitions out of it; The result is either an audit entries table (When / Who / Change / Domain / Entity / Record / Summary columns with change-type chips) plus a count, OR a valid 'No audit entries found' empty-state; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises OnRunQuery -> PermissionEngine.GetAuditTimeline, rendering a chronological Create/Update/Delete/Snapshot change log with per-type icons (ChangeIcon) and a 500-row cap notice. This timeline surface is a distinct code path from the two access-report tabs.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Explorer/dashboards/src/Permissions/audit-log-resource.component.ts (OnRunQuery, GetAuditTimeline, ChangeIcon, HasRunQuery) + audit-log-resource.component.html (selector-row Query button, perms-table When/Who/Change/Domain/Entity/Record/Summary, no-query and no-results empty-states)

- **[N152] Audit Log Reset Filters Clears Results** `(P2, permissions)`
  - **Goal:** You are logged in at http://localhost:4200. Open the Permissions app via the application switcher next to the logo, then click 'Audit Log' in the left nav. Pages can take 10-30s to load — wait, don't navigate away. First set at least one filter (for example pick a value in the 'Domain:' dropdown or set a 'From' date) and click 'Query' so results (or a no-results state) appear. Then click the 'Reset' button. Success is that Reset clears the filters back to their defaults ('All domains', 'Any user', empty dates) AND returns the surface to the 'No query yet' empty-state — the previously shown results table disappears. Seeing the filters and results reset is success.
  - **Pass criteria:** After running a query and then clicking 'Reset', the Domain/Changed-by/date filters return to their default (unset) values; The results area returns to the 'No query yet' empty-state (the prior entries table / no-results state is cleared); No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises OnResetFilters, which clears DomainFilter/UserFilter/StartDate/EndDate, empties Entries, and sets HasRunQuery=false — flipping the view back to the pre-query empty-state. This reset-to-initial-state branch is distinct from running a query.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Explorer/dashboards/src/Permissions/audit-log-resource.component.ts (OnResetFilters sets HasRunQuery=false and Entries=[]) + audit-log-resource.component.html (Reset button (click)="OnResetFilters()", @if (!HasRunQuery) 'No query yet' empty-state)

## Collaboration & Knowledge

### chat

- **[N162] Chat Workspace Load and Tab Chrome** `(P1, chat)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher dropdown next to the logo and choose the 'Chat' application. Wait for the conversations workspace to finish initializing (it shows a 'Loading agents...' spinner first). Confirm the workspace chrome renders: a top navigation bar with three tabs labeled 'Chats', 'Collections', and 'Tasks', a left sidebar containing a 'New Conversation' button, and either a conversation chat area or a welcome/empty state on the right. Seeing this three-tab workspace render is success. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away.

- **[N163] New Conversation Empty State** `(P1, chat)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher next to the logo, choose 'Chat', and wait for the workspace to load. Click the 'New Conversation' button in the left sidebar. Confirm the empty-state welcome renders in the main area: a 'Welcome to Conversations' greeting, one or more suggested prompt cards, a message composer input, and footer tips mentioning using '@AgentName' and pressing Enter to send. Seeing the welcome empty state with its composer is success — you do NOT need to send anything. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away.

- **[N164] Send Message And User Bubble Render** `(P1, chat)`
  - **Goal:** Open the application switcher, choose 'Chat', wait for the workspace, and start a new conversation. Type a short message such as 'Hello, what can you help me with?' into the composer at the bottom and press Enter (or click the send button). Confirm the message is accepted: your typed text appears as a user message bubble in the conversation thread, and the app shows it is working on a response (a processing/typing indicator, a spinning status on the message, or an AI response beginning to appear). Seeing your message posted and the app begin processing is success — you do NOT need to wait for the full agent answer. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away.
  - **Pass criteria:** The typed message posts and appears as a user message bubble in the thread; After sending, a processing/typing/working indicator or an AI response starting to render is visible; The composer clears or is disabled while processing (message was consumed, not left in the input); No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the full message-send round-trip (message-input onTextSubmitted -> conversation create -> message-list render + in-progress AI time-pill) — the primary write path of the chat feature.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Generic/conversations/src/lib/components/message/message-input.component.html (mj-ai-composer textSubmitted); conversation/conversation-chat-area.component.html (mj-conversation-message-list, isProcessing); message/message-item.component.html (time-pill in-progress, message-status)

Success is that the Pipeline Monitor renders its KPI strip and 'Recent Processing' feed, AND that clicking Run Pipeline puts the pipeline into a running/monitoring state (a progress bar with a stage label and percent appears, OR the run kicks off with pause/cancel controls) — you do NOT need to wait for the run to finish. Then click the 'Cron' type pill and confirm cron preset buttons and a cron expression input appear, with a human-readable cron description shown when an expression is set.   - **Pass criteria:** With Auto-sync enabled, selecting the Interval pill reveals interval preset buttons and a custom-minutes input; Selecting the Cron pill reveals cron preset buttons and a cron expression input (with a description when an expression is present); A Save button appears once the schedule configuration is changed; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the per-card schedule editor's reactive type-switching (SetScheduleType/SetInterval/SetCronExpression + GetCronDescription + HasChanges gating the Save button) — a bespoke in-card config editor unique to Schedules.

## Shell & Cross-cutting

### shell-crosscutting

- **[N300] Theme Toggle Light and Dark** `(P1, shell)`
  - **Goal:** You are logged in at http://localhost:4200. Click the user avatar button in the TOP-RIGHT corner of the header to open the user menu. Find the theme toggle row (it shows a sun/moon switch and a label like 'Theme' or 'Dark Mode') and click it to flip the theme. Verify the entire application visibly re-skins — for example a light interface switches to a dark background with light text (or vice-versa) — and the toggle switch reflects the new state. Then click the toggle again to flip it back. Seeing the whole shell change color scheme is success. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away.
  - **Pass criteria:** The user avatar menu opens and shows a theme toggle control (sun/moon switch); Clicking the toggle visibly changes the app's color scheme (light<->dark) across the header and page background; The toggle switch position/icon updates to reflect the newly applied theme; No error messages, blank screens, or crash pages
  - **Distinct:** Exercises the ThemeService.SetTheme round-trip and IsDarkMode re-skin wired through the dynamic user menu — a preference-application path not touched by any grid/form/query test.
  - **Grounding:** packages/Angular/Explorer/explorer-core/src/lib/shell/shell.component.html lines 201-212 (theme-toggle-item + theme-toggle-track); shell.component.ts onUserMenuItemClick 'select-theme-' branch calling themeService.SetTheme (lines 2208-2222) and get IsDarkMode (line 2290)

- **[N301] Command Palette Jump To Application** `(P1, shell)`
  - **Goal:** You are logged in at http://localhost:4200. Press Ctrl+/ (on Windows/Linux) or Cmd+/ (on Mac) to open the command palette (a centered modal search box with an application list). Type an application name such as 'Admin'. Verify the results list narrows to matching applications as you type. Then click the matching application (or press Enter with it highlighted) and confirm the app opens/loads. Seeing the palette filter to matches and navigate to the chosen app is success. Pages can take 10-30s to load in this resource-constrained environment — wait, don't navigate away.
